Figures
2
and
3
show how the rejection rates of
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
, YYS and LTM vary with the number of regressors under the null and alternative hypotheses. We consider Models 1-4 with
n=800
and
n=400
, respectively. We set the number of regressors
k_{n}=0,2,\cdots,80
(
k_{n}=0,1,\cdots,40)
for
n=800
(
n=400
), corresponding to
\kappa_{a,s}=0,0.01,\cdots,0.4
. The x-axis is
\kappa_{a,s}
. The y-axis is the rejection probabilities. We display only the figures under the SBR scheme. Similar patterns are found under the other randomization schemes, which are shown in the Online Supplement.

Panels (a) of the figures show that our methods (i.e.,
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and
\hat{\tau}^{*}
) have uniform size control over
\kappa_{a,s}
. Even when the dimensions of regressors vary from 0 to 80 (40) for
n=800
(
n=400
), our methods have close-to-nominal rejection rates under
H_{0}
, consistent with our theories. In contrast, YYS has rejection probabilities that increase linearly with
\kappa_{a.s}
in all models. Similarly, LTM displays this size distortion in Models 2 and 3, in which the sparsity condition fails.

Panels (b) of the figures show the power of the five methods under
H_{1}:\mu_{1}-\mu_{0}=0.2
. Several observations emerge. First, BCS’s power, our benchmark, does not vary with
\kappa_{a,s}
because it uses no regressors. Second, for all models,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
is always more powerful than BCS and usually more powerful than
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
, as our theory predicts. Third, as the number of regressors increases, the misspecification error decreases, and
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
becomes more powerful. However,
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
’s power drops even below that of the unadjusted estimator as
k_{n}
increases for Model 1. This reflects that many covariates do not always improve estimation accuracy, and the “no-harm” regression adjustments can in fact harm the estimation precision. Fourth, for Models 1-3,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
is generally more powerful than LTM as the dimension of regressors increases. For Model 4, LTM slightly outperforms
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
when
\kappa_{a,s}
is large for
n=800
due to the sparsity of the model.

6.3
Practical Implication of Dimensionality

When the dimension of covariates,
k_{n}
, is relatively small compared to
n^{1/2}
, our inference method remains valid and ”no-harm,” even when the linear regression adjustment is misspecified. This also holds for conventional inference methods like that of
Ye et al. 2022
.

When
k_{n}
exceeds
n^{1/2}
and the linear regression is approximately correctly specified (i.e., Assumption
2
(iii)), our inference method remains valid and “no-harm”, even when
k_{n}
is smaller but comparable to
n
, whereas the conventional method does not. Approximately correct specification is satisfied if the baseline covariates
Z_{i}
are fixed-dimensional and continuous, with
X_{i}
containing sieve basis functions of
Z_{i}
, or if the baseline covariates are categorical (discrete), with
X_{i}
including fully saturated dummies for all categories.

When
k_{n}
exceeds
n^{1/2}
and the linear regression adjustment is not approximately correctly specified, the adjusted and linear combination estimators,
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and
\hat{\tau}^{*}
, may exhibit non-negligible biases. These biases can potentially be corrected using the methods developed by
Lei and Ding 2021
and
Chiang
et al. 2023
, provided
k_{n}=o(n^{2/3})
. However, the statistical properties of these bias-corrected ATE estimators, and whether they remain ’no-harm’ under CARs, have not yet been studied.

When
k_{n}
exceeds
n^{2/3}
and the linear regression adjustment is not approximately correctly specified, there is currently no known method for valid causal inference using regression-adjusted estimators under any randomization scheme, which is left for future research.

7
Empirical Application

Studying the effects of child health and nutrition on educational outcomes helps us understand the connection between health and economic development in less developed countries (
(
Glewwe and
Miguel 2007
)
;
(
Dupas and
Miguel 2017
)
). To investigate the impact of an iron supplement program on students’ schooling attainment,
Chong et al. 2016
conducted a randomized experiment in Peru with a CAR framework.

Their experiment involved 215 students from a rural secondary school in Peru during the 2009 academic year. Students were stratified by five grade levels, with each grade having students randomly assigned to one of three groups: two treatment groups and one control group. In the treatments, one group viewed a video where a well-known soccer player endorsed iron supplements, and the other group saw a video with a doctor’s endorsement. The control group watched a video that did not mention iron supplements.

In this section, we focus on the impact of iron supplement program on the number of iron pills taken, whether a student is anemic at endline, and the student’s cognitive ability. Throughout the analysis, students in the two treatment arms are grouped into the treatment group as in
Chong et al. 2016
.

We use a fully saturated model, including two dummy covariates (gender and electricity at home) from the regressors in
Chong et al. 2016
and their interaction as the third covariate. This ensures that the model is correctly specified. Although the number of regressors is not large, it is still a significant proportion of the sample size given that the total sample size is not large either.
\kappa_{1,s}
range from approximately 8% to 15%, and
\kappa_{0,s}
range from about 16% to 30%.
9
9

9

We left out the strata where the number of observations in either treatment or control group is less than or equal to 4.

Table
3
presents the ATEs and the standard errors (in parentheses) estimated by different methods.
10
10

10

The description of these methods is similar to that in Section
6.2
.
We make three observations from the results. First, the variance estimates of the YYS method are smaller than those of
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
in general. For example, the standard error for pills taken is approximately about 17.6% lower with YYS compared to
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
, despite identical ATE estimates. This indicates that YYS method may fail to fully capture estimation errors that arise when using many regressors.

Second, the standard errors of
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
are consistently lower than those of both
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and BCS for all outcomes, with some differences being substantial. For example, for cognitive ability, the standard error of
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
is about 8.2% smaller than that of BCS. This result is expected because
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
is a weighted average of
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and the unadjusted estimator (BCS), designed to minimize asymptotic variance. The standard errors of
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
are also generally lower than those of LTM, though by a smaller margin.

Third, consistent with
Chong et al. 2016
, our results indicate that the iron supplement program increases the number of iron pills taken but has no significant effect on anemia or cognitive ability.

8
Conclusion

This paper shows that the estimation error of the “no-harm” regression adjustments can contaminate the ATE estimator and degrade estimation efficiency when the number of regressors is of the same order as the sample size. We then propose a new ATE estimator which is guaranteed to be weakly more efficient than both the estimators with and without RAs. We also propose a consistent estimator of its asymptotic variance and construct the corresponding Wald-statistic.

Appendix A
Verifying Assumption
3
for Gaussian Covariates

For
i\in\aleph_{s}
, we assume that
X_{i}=\Sigma_{s,n}^{1/2}Z_{i}
where
Z_{i}=(Z_{i,1},\cdots,Z_{i,k_{n}})
are
k_{n}
independent standard normal random variables and the covariance matrix
\Sigma_{s,n}\in\Re^{k_{n}\times k_{n}}
is symmetric and positive definite. The assumption that
Z_{i}
has zero mean is without loss of generality because the means will be canceled in the definition of
\breve{X}_{i}
. We focus on the case with
a=1
. By an abuse of notation, we assume
\aleph_{1,s}=\{1,\cdots,n_{1,s}\}
and
\aleph_{0,s}=\{1+n_{1,s},\cdots,n_{s}\}
and denote
Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}=(Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{n_{1,s}})^{\top}\in\Re^{n_{1,s}\times k_{n}}
and
Z_{\aleph_{s}}=(Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{n_{s}})^{\top}\in\Re^{n_{s}\times k_{n}}
. Then, we have

\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}=X_{\aleph_{1,s}}-1_{n_{1,s}}1_{n_{s}}^{\top}X_{\aleph_{s}}/n_{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}-1_{n_{a,s}}1_{n_{s}}^{\top}Z_{\aleph_{s}}/n_{s})\Sigma_{s,n}^{1/2}=\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s}}\Sigma_{s,n}^{1/2}

and

\displaystyle P_{1,s}=\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}\left[\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}^{\top}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}\right]^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s}}\left[Z_{\aleph_{s}}^{\top}\Gamma_{n}^{\top}\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s}}\right]^{-1}Z_{\aleph_{s}}^{\top}\Gamma_{n}^{\top},

where
\Gamma_{n}=(I_{n_{1,s}},0_{n_{1,s},n_{0,s}})-1_{n_{1,s}}1_{n_{s}}^{\top}/n_{s}
and
0_{n_{1,s},n_{0,s}}
is a
n_{1,s}\times n_{0,s}
matrix of zeros. For the
l
-th column of
\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s}}
denoted as
\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s},l}
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s},l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}W_{n}^{1/2}Z_{\aleph_{1,s},l},

where
Z_{\aleph_{1,s},l}
is an
n_{1,s}\times 1
standard normal vector and
W_{n}
is a
n_{1,s}\times n_{1,s}
matrix with
1-1/n_{s}
and
-1/n_{s}
being the diagonal and off-diagonal elements, respectively. Given that
Z_{\aleph_{s}}
are independent across columns, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}W_{n}^{1/2}Z_{\aleph_{1,s}},

which implies

\displaystyle 1-\gamma_{1,s,n}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s}}}{n_{1,s}}\left[\frac{Z_{\aleph_{s}}^{\top}\Gamma_{n}^{\top}\Gamma_{n}Z_{\aleph_{s}}}{n_{1,s}}\right]^{-1}\frac{Z_{\aleph_{s}}^{\top}\Gamma_{n}^{\top}1_{n_{1,s}}}{n_{1,s}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}W_{n}^{1/2}Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}}{n_{1,s}}\left[\frac{Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}^{\top}W_{n}Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}}{n_{1,s}}\right]^{-1}\frac{Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}^{\top}W_{n}^{1/2}1_{n_{1,s}}}{n_{1,s}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}U_{n}D_{n}^{1/2}Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}}{n_{1,s}}\left[\frac{Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}^{\top}D_{n}Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}}{n_{1,s}}\right]^{-1}\frac{Z_{\aleph_{1,s}}^{\top}D_{n}^{1/2}U_{n}^{\top}1_{n_{1,s}}}{n_{1,s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-n_{1,s}/n_{s})}{n_{1,s}}Z_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in[n_{1,s}-1]}Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\top}+\left(\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\right)Z_{n_{1,s}}Z_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\right]^{-1}Z_{n_{1,s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-n_{1,s}/n_{s})}{n_{1,s}}Z_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\left[\mathcal{A}_{n}^{-1}-\frac{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{-1}(\left(\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\right)Z_{n_{1,s}}Z_{n_{1,s}}^{\top})\mathcal{A}_{n}^{-1}}{1+\left(\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\right)Z_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{-1}Z_{n_{1,s}}}\right]Z_{n_{1,s}},

(A.1)

where
W_{n}=U_{n}D_{n}U_{n}^{\top}
is the eigenvalue decomposition of
W_{n}
,
D_{n}=\text{diag}(1,\cdots,1,1-n_{1,s}/n_{s})
is the eigenvalues,
U_{n}
is the corresponding eigenvectors,
\mathcal{A}_{n}=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in[n_{1,s}-1]}Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\top}
, the fourth equality is by
1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}U_{n}=(0,\cdots,0,\sqrt{n_{1,s}})
, and the last equality follows the Sherman-Morrison matrix inverse formula. Consider the eigenvalue decomposition of
\frac{n_{1,s}}{n_{1,s}-1}\mathcal{A}_{n}=\mathcal{U}_{n}\Lambda_{n}\mathcal{U}_{n}^{\top}
where
\Lambda_{n}=\text{diag}(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{k_{n}})
. We note that
Z_{n_{1,s}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, and thus,
\mathcal{U}_{n}
and
\Lambda_{n}
, are independent by construction. Then, for any bounded and smooth function
f:\Re^{k_{n}}\mapsto\Re
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(\mathcal{U}_{n}^{\top}Z_{n_{1,s}}\mid\Lambda_{n}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}(f(\mathcal{U}_{n}^{\top}Z_{n_{1,s}}\mid\mathcal{U}_{n},\Lambda_{n})\mid\Lambda_{n}]=\mathbb{E}f(Z_{n_{1,s}})=\mathbb{E}f(\mathcal{U}_{n}^{\top}Z_{n_{1,s}}),

where we use the fact that the distribution of
Z_{n_{1,s}}
(
k_{n}\times 1
standard normal random vector) is invariant to rotations. This implies
\mathcal{U}_{n}^{\top}Z_{n_{1,s}}
and
\Lambda_{n}
are independent. Denote
\mathcal{U}_{n}^{\top}Z_{n_{1,s}}
as
\mathcal{G}=(g_{1},\cdots,g_{K_{n}})^{\top}
which are independent of
\Lambda_{n}
and suppose
k_{n}/n_{1,s}\rightarrow\kappa_{1,s}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}Z_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\left(\frac{n_{1,s}}{n_{1,s}-1}\mathcal{A}_{n}\right)^{-1}Z_{n_{1,s}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{l=1}^{k_{n}}\lambda_{l}^{-1}g_{l}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{l=1}^{k_{n}}\lambda_{l}^{-1}+\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{l=1}^{k_{n}}\lambda_{l}^{-1}(g_{l}^{2}-1)

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{n}}{n_{1,s}}\frac{1}{k_{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{k_{n}}\lambda_{l}^{-1}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\int_{\lambda_{-}}^{\lambda_{+}}\frac{\sqrt{(\lambda_{+}-\lambda)(\lambda-\lambda_{-})}}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}d\lambda,

(A.2)

where
\lambda_{\pm}=(1\pm\sqrt{\kappa_{1,s}})^{2}
. We note that the third equality in (
A
) holds because
\lambda_{k_{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}1-\sqrt{\kappa_{1,s}}
which is bounded away from zero,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{l=1}^{k_{n}}\lambda_{l}^{-1}(g_{l}^{2}-1)\mid\Lambda_{n}\right)=0,

and

\displaystyle Var\left(\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{l=1}^{k_{n}}\lambda_{l}^{-1}(g_{l}^{2}-1)\mid\Lambda_{n}\right)\leq\frac{k_{n}}{n_{1,s}^{2}\lambda_{k_{n}}^{2}}\rightarrow 0,

and the last convergence in probability (
A
) holds by the Marčenko-Pastur theorem (see, for example,
Bai 2008
). Denote
\int_{\lambda_{-}}^{\lambda_{+}}\frac{\sqrt{(\lambda_{+}-\lambda)(\lambda-\lambda_{-})}}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}d\lambda
as
\zeta_{1,s}
, then we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}Z_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{-1}Z_{n_{1,s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\int_{\lambda_{-}}^{\lambda_{+}}\frac{\sqrt{(\lambda_{+}-\lambda)(\lambda-\lambda_{-})}}{2\pi\lambda^{2}}d\lambda,

and thus, by (
A
),

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,s,n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{1+(1-\pi_{s})\zeta_{1,s}}\equiv\gamma_{1,s,\infty}.

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
3.1

Denote
\hat{\tau}_{s}=\hat{\tau}_{1,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0,s}
,
\tau_{a,s}=\mathbb{E}(Y(a)|S=s)
for
a=0,1
, and
\tau_{s}=\mathbb{E}(Y(1)-Y(0)|S=s)
. Note that

\displaystyle Y_{i}(1)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1,S_{i}}+X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,S_{i}}+e_{i,S_{i}}(1)+\varepsilon_{i}(1)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1,S_{i}}+\overline{X}_{S_{i}}^{\top}\beta_{1,S_{i}}+\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,S_{i}}+e_{i,S_{i}}(1)+\varepsilon_{i}(1),

which implies

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{1,s}

\displaystyle=(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}1_{n_{1,s}})^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}Y_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}+(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}1_{n_{1,s}})^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}(e_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1)+\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1)))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\left(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-e_{i,s}(1)\right)

\displaystyle+(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}1_{n_{1,s}})^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}(e_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1)+\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))).

By Assumption
2
(iv), we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}e_{i,s}(1)\right|\leq\left(\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}e_{i,s}^{2}(1)\right)^{1/2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

and

\displaystyle\left|n_{1,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}e_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))\right|

\displaystyle\leq n_{1,s}^{-1}||M_{1,s}||_{op}||1_{n_{1,s}}||_{2}||e_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1)||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}e_{i,s}^{2}(1)\right)^{1/2}=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

This implies the linear expansion

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{1,s}-\tau_{1,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\left(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s)\right)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}n_{1,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{0,s}-\tau_{0,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\left(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s)\right)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{0,s,n}^{-1}n_{0,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{0,s}}^{\top}M_{0,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0))+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

and thus

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{s}-\tau_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\left[\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\tau_{s}\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}n_{1,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))-\gamma_{0,s,n}^{-1}n_{0,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{0,s}}^{\top}M_{0,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0))+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{adj}-\tau

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\left[\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s}^{-1}n_{1,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))-\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s}^{-1}n_{0,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{0,s}}^{\top}M_{0,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0))\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\hat{p}_{s}}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\left[\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s)\right]

\displaystyle-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\hat{p}_{s}}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\left[\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i})-\tau\right]+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{unadj}-\tau

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\hat{p}_{s}}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}\varepsilon_{i}(1)-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\hat{p}_{s}}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}\varepsilon_{i}(0)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\hat{p}_{s}}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}A_{i}1\{S_{i}=s\}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s))

\displaystyle-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{\hat{p}_{s}}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-A_{i})1\{S_{i}=s\}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i})-\tau).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\tau}^{adj}-\tau\\
\hat{\tau}^{unadj}-\tau\end{pmatrix}=\mathcal{U}_{n}+\mathcal{V}_{n}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{W}_{n}\\
\mathcal{W}_{n}\end{pmatrix}+o_{P}(1),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}n_{1,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))-\gamma_{0,s,n}^{-1}n_{0,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{0,s}}^{\top}M_{0,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0))\\
n_{1,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))-n_{0,s}^{-1}(1_{n_{0,s}}^{\top}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0))\end{pmatrix},

(B.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{n}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in[n]}1\{S_{i}=s\}\left(\phi_{i}(1)-\phi_{i}(0)\right)\\
\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\phi_{i}(1)-\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\phi_{i}(0)\end{pmatrix},

(B.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i})-\tau),

(B.3)

and
\phi_{i}(a)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i})
. Then, the desired result holds by Lemma
G.1
.

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
4.1

We note that

\displaystyle\hat{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\Sigma_{2,2}-\Sigma_{1,2}}{\Sigma_{1,1}+\Sigma_{2,2}-2\Sigma_{1,2}}=\argmin_{w}(w,1-w)\Sigma(w,1-w)^{\top}.

This implies
\hat{\tau}^{*}
is weakly more efficient than both
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
which correspond to
\hat{w}=1
and 0, respectively. Then, by the continuous mapping theorem, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left[(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})\hat{\Sigma}(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})^{\top}\right]^{-1/2}(\hat{\tau}^{*}-\tau)\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Appendix D
Proof of Corollary
4.1

For the weak convergence, we note that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{*}-\tau_{0})=(\hat{\omega},1-\hat{\omega})\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{N}_{1}\\
\hat{N}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\rightsquigarrow(\omega^{*},1-\omega^{*})\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}N_{1}\\
N_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

Recall
(a,b)=(1,1)\Sigma^{-1/2}
. Given that both
N_{1}^{*}=\frac{aN_{1}+bN_{2}}{\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}}
and the limit distribution of

\sqrt{n}\left[(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})\hat{\Sigma}(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})^{\top}\right]^{-1/2}(\hat{\tau}^{*}-\tau_{0})
have variance 1, it suffices to show that
a/b=\tilde{a}/\tilde{b}
where
(\tilde{a},\tilde{b})=(\omega^{*},1-\omega^{*})\Sigma^{1/2}
and
\omega^{*}=(\Sigma_{2,2}-\Sigma_{1,2})/(\Sigma_{1,1}+\Sigma_{2,2}-2\Sigma_{1,2})
. To see this, we note that

\displaystyle\Sigma^{1/2}=\frac{1}{t}\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{1,1}+\eta&\Sigma_{1,2}\\
\Sigma_{1,2}&\Sigma_{2,2}+\eta\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\Sigma^{-1/2}=\frac{t}{(\Sigma_{1,1}+\eta)(\Sigma_{2,2}+\eta)-\Sigma^{2}_{1,2}}\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{2,2}+\eta&-\Sigma_{1,2}\\
-\Sigma_{1,2}&\Sigma_{1,1}+\eta\end{pmatrix},

where
\eta=\sqrt{\Sigma_{1,1}\Sigma_{2,2}-\Sigma_{1,2}^{2}}
and
t=\sqrt{\Sigma_{1,1}+\Sigma_{2,2}+2\eta}
. Then, it is obvious that

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\frac{t(\Sigma_{2,2}-\Sigma_{1,2}+\eta)}{(\Sigma_{1,1}+\eta)(\Sigma_{2,2}+\eta)-\Sigma^{2}_{1,2}},

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\frac{t(\Sigma_{1,1}-\Sigma_{1,2}+\eta)}{(\Sigma_{1,1}+\eta)(\Sigma_{1,1}+\eta)-\Sigma^{2}_{1,2}},

\displaystyle\tilde{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\Sigma_{2,2}-\Sigma_{1,2})(\Sigma_{1,1}+\eta)+(\Sigma_{1,1}-\Sigma_{1,2})\Sigma_{1,2}}{t(\Sigma_{1,1}+\Sigma_{2,2}-2\Sigma_{1,2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\Sigma_{2,2}\Sigma_{1,1}-\Sigma_{1,2}^{2})+\eta(\Sigma_{2,2}-\Sigma_{1,2})}{t(\Sigma_{1,1}+\Sigma_{2,2}-2\Sigma_{1,2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(\eta+\Sigma_{2,2}-\Sigma_{1,2})}{t(\Sigma_{1,1}+\Sigma_{2,2}-2\Sigma_{1,2})},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(\eta+\Sigma_{1,1}-\Sigma_{1,2})}{t(\Sigma_{1,1}+\Sigma_{2,2}-2\Sigma_{1,2})},

which implies
a/b=\tilde{a}/\tilde{b}
.

For the optimality result, note that

\displaystyle(\mathbb{W}_{n},\psi(\hat{N}_{1},\hat{N}_{2}))\rightsquigarrow(1\{(N^{*})^{2}\geq\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\},\psi(N_{1},N_{2})),

where
(N_{1},N_{2})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}a\Delta\\
b\Delta\end{pmatrix},I_{2}\right)
and
N^{*}=\frac{aN_{1}+bN_{2}}{\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}}
. Consider a limit experiment in which a researcher observes
(N_{1},N_{2})
,
knows the values of
(a,b)
, and wants to test
\Delta=0
versus the two-sided alternative.
Lehmann and
Romano 2006
show
1\{(N^{*})^{2}\geq\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\}
is the UMP unbiased test for this limit experiment, implying that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}1\{(N^{*})^{2}\geq\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\}\geq\mathbb{E}\psi(N_{1},N_{2})

for any
\psi\in\Psi_{\alpha}^{U}.
This implies

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{W}_{n}=\mathbb{E}1\{(N^{*})^{2}\geq\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\}=\sup_{\psi\in\Psi_{\alpha}^{U}}\mathbb{E}\psi(N_{1},N_{2})=\sup_{\psi\in\Psi_{\alpha}^{U}}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\psi(\hat{N}_{1},\hat{N}_{2}).

The inequality that

\displaystyle\sup_{\psi\in\Psi_{\alpha}^{U}}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\psi(\hat{N}_{1},\hat{N}_{2})\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\breve{\psi}_{n}

holds due to the uniform integrability of
\breve{\psi}_{n}
, the fact that
\breve{\psi}_{n}=\psi(\hat{N}_{1},\hat{N}_{2})+o_{P}(1)
for some
\psi\in\Psi^{U}_{\alpha}
, and the dominated convergence theorem.

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
4.2

We derive the limits of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{U}}
,
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{V}}
, and
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{W}}
in the following three steps.

Step 1: Limit of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{U}}
. Following
Bugni
et al. 2018
, we define
\{(X_{i}^{s},\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1),\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(0)):1\leq i\leq n\}
as a sequence of i.i.d. random variables with marginal distributions equal to the distribution of
(X_{i},\varepsilon_{i}(1),\varepsilon_{i}(0))|S_{i}=s
and
N_{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}<s\}
. We order units by strata and then by
A_{i}=1
first and
A_{i}=0
second within each stratum. This means for the
s
th stratum, units indexed from
N_{s}+1
to
N_{s}+n_{1,s}
are treated and units indexed from
N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1
to
N_{s}+n_{s}
are untreated. Then, we have
(Y_{i}(a)\mid S_{i}=s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}Y_{i}^{s}(a)
and
(\tilde{e}_{i}(a)\mid S_{i}=s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}e_{i,s}(a)
where

\displaystyle Y_{i}^{s}(a)=\alpha_{a,s}+(X_{i}^{s})^{\top}\beta_{a,s}+\tilde{e}_{i}^{s}(a)+\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(a)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{e}_{i}^{s}(a)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i}=x,S_{i}=s)-\alpha_{a,s}-(X_{i}^{s})^{\top}\beta_{a,s}.

We further define
\tilde{M}_{a,s,i,j}
as the
(i,j)
th entry of the
n_{a,s}\times n_{a,s}
matrix,

\tilde{M}_{a,s}=I_{n_{a,s}}-\Xi_{a,s}(\Xi_{a,s}^{\top}\Xi_{a,s})^{-1}\Xi_{a,s}^{\top}
,
\Xi_{1,s}=((\breve{X}_{N_{s}+1}^{s})^{\top},\cdots,(\breve{X}_{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}^{s})^{\top})^{\top}
is an
n_{1,s}\times k_{n}
matrix,
\Xi_{0,s}=((\breve{X}_{N_{s}+1+n_{1,s}}^{s})^{\top},\cdots,(\breve{X}_{N_{s}+n_{s}}^{s})^{\top})^{\top}
is an
n_{0,s}\times k_{n}
matrix,

\displaystyle\breve{X}_{i}^{s}=X_{i}^{s}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}X_{j}^{s},\quad\text{if}\quad N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{s},

\tilde{\gamma}_{a,s,n}=1_{n_{a,s}}^{\top}\tilde{M}_{a,s}1_{n_{a,s}}
, and
\phi_{i}^{s}(a)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)
.

Step 1.1: Limit of
\hat{\omega}_{a,s}^{2}
.
We consider the case with
a=1
. The result for
a=0
can be established in the same manner. Given the above definitions, we see that, conditionally on
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)})
, and thus, unconditionally,

\displaystyle\hat{\omega}_{1,s}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left(\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,j}\right)^{2}Y_{i}^{s}(1)\tilde{\acute{\varepsilon}}_{1,s,i},

where
\tilde{\acute{\varepsilon}}_{1,s,i}=\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1,s,i}/\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}
and
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1,s,i}
is the residual from the linear regression of
Y_{i}^{s}(1)
on
(1,\breve{X}_{i}^{s})
with observations
N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}
. Because
\{(X_{i}^{s},Y_{i}^{s}(1)):1\leq i\leq n\}
are i.i.d. and independent of
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)})
we can directly apply
Jochmans 2022
and conclude that

\displaystyle\hat{\omega}_{1,s}^{2}-\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}\sigma_{1,s,n}^{2}=o_{P}(1).

Then, because
\tilde{\gamma}_{a,s,n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\gamma_{a,s,n}
and Assumption
3
holds, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\omega}_{1,s}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\omega_{1,s,\infty}^{2}.

Step 1.2: Limit of
\hat{\varpi}_{a,s}
.
Again, we focus on
\hat{\varpi}_{1,s}
. Following the argument in
Step 1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\varpi}_{1,s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left(\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,j}\right)Y_{i}^{s}(1)\tilde{\acute{\varepsilon}}_{1,s,i}.

Note that

\displaystyle\tilde{\acute{\varepsilon}}_{1,s,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}(Y_{k}^{s}(1)-\tilde{\tau}_{1,s})}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}(\tilde{e}_{k}^{s}(1)+\alpha_{1,s}+\widetilde{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\tilde{\tau}_{1,s}+\varepsilon_{k}^{s}(1))}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}},

where
\tilde{\tau}_{1,s}
is the intercept of the OLS regression of
Y_{i}^{s}(1)
on
(1,\breve{X}_{i}^{s})
using observations
N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}
and
\widetilde{X}_{s}=\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}X_{i}^{s}
. By construction, we have
\tilde{\tau}_{1,s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\hat{\tau}_{1,s}
. Let
\theta_{i}=\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left(\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,j}\right)Y_{i}^{s}(1)\tilde{\acute{\varepsilon}}_{1,s,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}Y_{i}^{s}(1)\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}\varepsilon_{k}^{s}(1)}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}+\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}Y_{i}^{s}(1)\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}\tilde{e}_{k}^{s}(1)}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}^{2}Y_{i}^{s}(1)}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}(\alpha_{1,s}+\widetilde{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\tilde{\tau}_{1,s}))

\displaystyle\equiv T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}.

For
T_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle|T_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}^{2}(Y_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}^{2}}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}(\tilde{e}_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}\frac{\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}|\theta_{i}|}{\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}(Y_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}(\tilde{e}_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1),

where the first inequality is by
||\tilde{M}_{1,s}||_{op}\leq 1
and the last equality holds because by Assumption
2
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\in\aleph_{1,s}}M_{1,s,i,i}\geq\delta>0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}(\tilde{e}_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}e_{i,s}^{2}(1)=o_{P}(n^{-1}),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}|\theta_{i}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\max_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}|\sum_{j\in\aleph_{1,s}}M_{1,s,i,j}|=o_{P}(n^{1/2}).

For
T_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|T_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}^{4}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}^{2}}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}(Y_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\right]^{1/2}|\alpha_{1,s}+\widetilde{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\tilde{\tau}_{1,s}|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}\frac{\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}|\theta_{i}|}{\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\left[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}(Y_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\right]^{1/2}|\alpha_{1,s}+\widetilde{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\tilde{\tau}_{1,s})|

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1),

where the last equality holds because

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,j}=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\tilde{M}_{1,s}1_{n_{1,s}}\leq||\tilde{M}_{1,s}||_{op}\leq 1

and

\displaystyle|\alpha_{1,s}+\widetilde{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\tilde{\tau}_{1,s})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}|\alpha_{1,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\hat{\tau}_{1,s})|

\displaystyle=|(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}1_{n_{1,s}})^{-1}(1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}M_{1,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1))|+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=O_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

Next, we focus on
T_{1}
. Denote
\mu_{i}^{s}(1)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}^{s}(1)|X_{i}^{s})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}\mu_{i}^{s}(1)M_{1,s,i,k}\varepsilon_{k}^{s}(1)}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{N_{s}+1\leq k\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s},k\neq i}\frac{\theta_{i}\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1)M_{1,s,i,k}\varepsilon_{k}^{s}(1)}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}[(\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}-\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}]+\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv T_{1,1}+T_{1,2}+T_{1,3}+T_{1,4},

where
\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[(\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}|X_{i}^{s}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)
. Recall
\Xi_{1,s}=((\breve{X}_{N_{s}+1}^{s})^{\top},\cdots,(\breve{X}_{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}^{s})^{\top})^{\top}
. We have
\mathbb{E}(T_{1,1}\mid\Xi_{1,s})=0
and

\displaystyle Var(T_{1,1}\mid\Xi_{1,s})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}}\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{V}_{1,s,k}^{2}\left[\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}\mu_{i}^{s}(1)M_{1,s,i,k}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}}{n_{1,s}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}}\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left[\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}\mu_{i}^{s}(1)M_{1,s,i,k}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}\right]\left[\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{j}\mu_{j}^{s}(1)M_{1,s,j,k}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,j,j}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}}{n_{1,s}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left[\frac{\theta_{i}\mu_{i}^{s}(1)\theta_{j}\mu_{j}^{s}(1)M_{1,s,i,j}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}\tilde{M}_{1,s,j,j}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}}{n_{1,s}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left[\frac{\theta_{i}\mu_{i}^{s}(1)}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}}{n_{1,s}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}^{2}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}(\mu_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\right]\left[\frac{\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}}{n_{1,s}}\right]

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1),

where the second equality holds because the matrix
\tilde{M}_{1,s}
is idempotent, the second inequality holds because
||\tilde{M}_{1,s}||_{op}\leq 1
, and the last equality holds because by Assumption
2
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\in\aleph_{1,s}}M_{1,s,i,i}\geq\delta>0,\quad\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}=O_{P}(1),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}|\theta_{i}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\max_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\left|\sum_{j\in\aleph_{1,s}}M_{1,s,i,j}\right|=o_{P}(n^{1/2}).

This implies
T_{1,1}=o_{P}(1)
.

For
T_{1,2}
, we have
\mathbb{E}(T_{1,2}\mid\Xi_{1,s})=0
and

\displaystyle Var(T_{1,2}\mid\Xi_{1,s})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{1,s}^{2}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{N_{s}+1\leq k\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s},k\neq i}\frac{\theta_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}^{2}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}\tilde{V}_{1,s,k}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n_{1,s}^{2}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{N_{s}+1\leq k\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s},k\neq i}\frac{|\theta_{i}\theta_{k}|\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}^{2}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}\tilde{V}_{1,s,k}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{4}}{n_{1,s}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{\theta_{i}^{2}\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\max_{i\in[n]}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{4}}{n_{1,s}^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\sum_{k=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\frac{|\theta_{i}\theta_{k}|\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,k}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left[\frac{\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{4}\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}}{n_{1,s}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{2}\min_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,i}}\right]

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1),

where the last equality is by
\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}=o_{P}(n)
.

For
T_{1,3}
, we have
\mathbb{E}(T_{1,3}\mid\Xi_{1,s})=0
and

\displaystyle Var(T_{1,3}\mid\Xi_{1,s})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}}{n_{1,s}^{2}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left([(\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}-\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}]^{2}\mid\Xi_{1,s}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\mathbb{E}\left([(\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}-\tilde{V}_{1,s,i}^{2}]^{2}\mid\Xi_{1,s}\right)}{n_{1,s}^{2}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}=O_{P}(n^{-1}),

where we use the fact that
\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\theta_{i}^{2}=1_{n_{1,s}}^{\top}\tilde{M}_{1,s}1_{n_{1,s}}\leq n_{1,s}
. This implies
T_{1,3}=o_{P}(1)
.

Last, by Assumption
3
, we have

\displaystyle T_{1,4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{1,s}}M_{1,s,i,j})\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\varpi_{a,s,\infty}.

This concludes the proof of
Step 1.2.

Step 1.3: Consistency of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{U}}
.
We note that
n_{s}^{2}/(nn_{1,s})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}p_{s}/\pi_{s}
and
n_{s}^{2}/(nn_{0,s})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}p_{s}/(1-\pi_{s})
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{U}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\omega_{1,s,\infty}^{2}}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{\omega_{0,s,\infty}^{2}}{1-\pi_{s}}&\frac{\omega_{1,s,\infty}^{2}}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{\omega_{0,s,\infty}^{2}}{1-\pi_{s}}\\
\frac{\omega_{1,s,\infty}^{2}}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{\omega_{0,s,\infty}^{2}}{1-\pi_{s}}&\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Step 2: Limit of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{V}}^{adj}
.
It suffices to consider the limits of
(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i})/n_{a,s}
and
\hat{\beta}_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\hat{\beta}_{0,s}/n_{s}
, which are derived in the following two steps.

Step 2.1: Limit of
(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i})/n_{a,s}
.
We note that

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{a,s}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{a,s}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}(Y_{i}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s))

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{a,s}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left[(\alpha_{a,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s))+\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}+e_{i,s}(a)+\varepsilon_{i}(a)\right]

\displaystyle=\beta_{a,s}+\Gamma_{a,s}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(a)+\Gamma_{a,s}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left[(\alpha_{a,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s))+e_{i,s}(a)\right].

(E.1)

By (
E.1
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}

\displaystyle=\beta_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\beta_{a,s}+\left(\varepsilon_{\aleph_{a,s}}^{\top}(a)P_{a,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{a,s}}(a)-\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}\right)+2\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}\varepsilon_{i}(a)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}(\alpha_{a,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s))+\varepsilon_{\aleph_{a,s}}^{\top}(a)P_{a,s}\left[1_{n_{a,s}}(\alpha_{a,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s))+e_{\aleph_{a,s}}(a)\right]

\displaystyle+\left[1_{n_{a,s}}(\alpha_{a,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s))+e_{\aleph_{a,s}}(a)\right]^{\top}P_{a,s}\left[1_{n_{a,s}}(\alpha_{a,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s))+e_{\aleph_{a,s}}(a)\right]

\displaystyle\equiv\beta_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\beta_{a,s}+\sum_{l\in[5]}I_{l},

(E.2)

where
e_{\aleph_{a,s}}(a)
and
\varepsilon_{\aleph_{a,s}}(a)
are
n_{a,s}\times 1
vectors of
\{e_{i,s}(a)\}_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}
and
\{\varepsilon_{i,s}(a)\}_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}
, respectively. We note that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-e_{i,s}(a)-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{s}}\left(\mathbb{E}(Y_{j}(a)|X_{j},S_{j}=s)-e_{j,s}(a)\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-e_{i,s}(a)\right]^{2}+\frac{n_{a,s}}{n_{s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{s}}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}(Y_{j}(a)|X_{j},S_{j}=s)-e_{j,s}(a)\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(n),

(E.3)

\displaystyle\left|\left[\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\beta_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\beta_{a,s}\right]^{1/2}-\left[\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s))^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left[\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s})^{2}\right]^{1/2}-\left[\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s))^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left(e_{i,s}(a)-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{s}}e_{j,s}(a)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}+\left|\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{s}}\mathbb{E}(Y_{j}(a)|X_{j},S_{j}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)\right|

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1),

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s))^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}var(\phi_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s),

where
\phi_{i}(a)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)
. This implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\beta_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\beta_{a,s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}var(\phi_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s).

Next, we show
I_{1},\cdots,I_{5}
are
o_{P}(n)
. Lemma
G.2
shows
I_{1}=o_{P}(n)
. For
I_{2}
, we note that, conditional on
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})
,
\{\varepsilon_{i}(a)\}_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}
is independent across
i
and mean zero, which implies

\displaystyle Var\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}\varepsilon_{i}(a)\mid A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(a)\mid A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right]\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s})^{2}\right]=O_{P}(n),

where the equality is by (
E
). Therefore, we have
I_{2}=O_{P}(n^{1/2})=o_{P}(n)
. Because
\alpha_{a,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}-\hat{\tau}_{a}(s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}o_{P}(1)
by the proof of Theorem
3.1
,
\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}e_{i,s}^{2}(a)=o(n)
, and
\lambda_{\max}(P_{a,s})\leq 1
, we have
I_{l}=o_{P}(n)
for
l=3,4,5
. This means

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\left(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}var(\phi_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s).

Step 2.2: Limit of
\hat{\beta}_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\hat{\beta}_{0,s}/n_{s}
.
By (
E.1
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\hat{\beta}_{0,s}

\displaystyle=\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\beta_{0,s}+\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(0)

\displaystyle+\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left(\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)+e_{i,s}(0)\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(1)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\beta_{0,s}+\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(1)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(0)

\displaystyle+\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(1)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left(\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)+e_{i,s}(0)\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left(\alpha_{1,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\hat{\tau}_{1}(s)+e_{i,s}(1)\right)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\beta_{0,s}

\displaystyle+\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left(\alpha_{1,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\hat{\tau}_{1}(s)+e_{i,s}(1)\right)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(0)

\displaystyle+\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left(\alpha_{1,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}-\hat{\tau}_{1}(s)+e_{i,s}(1)\right)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\left(\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)+e_{i,s}(0)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{l\in[9]}I_{l}.

Following the previous argument, we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{I_{1}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{s}}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s))(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s))+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}cov(\phi_{i}(1),\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s).

For
I_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(I_{2}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=0

and

\displaystyle var(I_{2}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\lesssim\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\beta_{1,s}\max_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(0)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=o_{P}(n^{2}),

where we use the facts that (
E
) holds and

\displaystyle\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\beta_{1,s}\leq\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}||\breve{X}_{\aleph_{s}}\beta_{1,s}||_{2}^{2}=o_{P}(n^{2}).

(E.4)

This implies
I_{2}/n=o_{P}(1)
.

For
I_{3}
, by Cauchy’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle|I_{3}|\leq\left\|\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\times O_{P}(1)=o_{P}(n),

where the first inequality holds by Assumption
2
(iv) and the facts that

\displaystyle\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)=O_{P}(n^{-1/2}),\quad\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}e_{i,s}^{2}(a)=o_{P}(1)

and the equality holds by Assumption
4
, (
E.4
), and the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}=\left(\beta_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\beta_{1,s}\right)^{1/2}=o_{P}(n).

We can show
I_{4}=o_{P}(n)
following the same argument used to bound
I_{2}
.

For
I_{5}
, we note that the two index sets
\aleph_{0,s}
and
\aleph_{1,s}
do not overlap. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(I_{5}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=0

and

\displaystyle var(I_{5}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\leq\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{0,s}}B_{i,j}^{2}\left[\max_{a=0,1}\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(0)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right]^{2}=o_{P}(n^{2}),

where we have
B_{i,j}=\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{j}
and

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{0,s}}B_{i,j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\text{trace}(\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{i})

\displaystyle=\text{trace}(\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2})

\displaystyle=\text{trace}(\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2})+\text{trace}(\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2})=o_{P}(n^{2}),

where we use the fact that
\Gamma_{s}=\Gamma_{0,s}+\Gamma_{1,s}
.

For
I_{6}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{6}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(1)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right]\right||\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)|

\displaystyle+\left|\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(1)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}e_{i,s}(0)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\equiv|I_{6,1}||\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)|+|I_{6,2}|.

We note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(I_{6,1}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=0

and

\displaystyle var(I_{6,1}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})

\displaystyle\leq\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right]\left[\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right]

\displaystyle=o_{P}(n^{3}).

where the second last equality holds because

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}S_{0}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\leq n_{0,s}\left\|\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}S_{0}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}\right\|_{op}

\displaystyle\leq n_{0,s}\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n_{0,s}\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}=o_{P}(n^{3}).

This implies
|I_{6,1}||\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)|=o_{P}(n)
because
|\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s)|=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
. Similarly, for
I_{6,2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(I_{6,2}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=0

and

\displaystyle var(I_{6,2}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})

\displaystyle\leq\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}e_{i,s}(0)\right]^{\top}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}S_{0}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\breve{X}_{i}e_{i,s}(0)\right]\left[\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}S_{0}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}\right\|_{op}\left\|e_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0)\right\|_{2}^{2}\left[\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right]=o_{P}(n^{2}),

where we use the fact that
\left\|e_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0)\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{P}(1)
. This implies
I_{6}=o_{P}(n)
.

We can show
I_{7}=o_{P}(n)
and
I_{8}=o_{P}(n)
following the same argument used to bound
I_{3}
and
I_{6}
, respectively.

For
I_{9}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{9}|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}\right\|_{op}\left\|1_{n_{1,s}}(\alpha_{1,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}-\hat{\tau}_{1}(s))+e_{\aleph_{1,s}}(1)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\times\left\|1_{n_{0,s}}(\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s))+e_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0)\right\|_{2}=o_{P}(n),

where we use the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{0,s}}\right\|_{op}

\displaystyle=\left\|\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}^{\top}\right\|_{op}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\breve{X}_{\aleph_{1,s}}\right\|_{op}^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left[\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\Gamma_{1,s}^{-1}\Gamma_{s}^{1/2}\right\|_{op}\right]^{1/2}=o_{P}(n)

and

\displaystyle\left\|1_{n_{0,s}}(\alpha_{0,s}+\overline{X}_{s}^{\top}-\hat{\tau}_{0}(s))+e_{\aleph_{0,s}}(0)\right\|_{2}=O_{P}(1).

This leads to the desired result in this step that
\hat{\beta}_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\hat{\beta}_{0,s}/n_{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}cov(\phi_{i}(1),\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s)
.

Step 3: Limit of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{W}}
.

For
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{W}}
, by the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we have
\hat{\tau}_{1,s}-\hat{\tau}_{0,s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{E}(Y(1)-Y(0)|S=s)
and
\hat{\tau}^{adj}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\tau
. By Assumption
1
, we also have
\hat{p}_{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}p_{s}
, which implies the desired result.

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
5.1

This proof depends on the results of Lemma
G.3
. Let
S^{k_{n}}
be the unit sphere in
\Re^{k_{n}}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s}f_{i}=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}f_{i}
for any function of observations
f_{i}
,
\mathbb{P}_{a,s}f_{i}=\mathbb{E}(f_{i}|A_{i}=a,S_{i}=s)=\mathbb{E}(f_{i}|S_{i}=s)
,
||\cdot||_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}
be the
L_{2}
norm w.r.t. the probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s}
.

Step 1: Asymptotic normality and the expression of
\Omega
.
Following Remark
3
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{adj}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{A_{i}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1,S_{i}})}{\hat{\pi}_{S_{i}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{(1-A_{i})(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{0,S_{i}})}{1-\hat{\pi}_{S_{i}}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{1,S_{i}}-\hat{\beta}_{0,S_{i}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{A_{i}(Y_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*})}{\hat{\pi}_{S_{i}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{(1-A_{i})(Y_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*})}{1-\hat{\pi}_{S_{i}}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*}-\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*})

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\left(\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}X_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{s}}X_{i}\right)^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\hat{\beta}_{1,s})

\displaystyle-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\left(\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}X_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{s}}X_{i}\right)^{\top}(\beta_{0,s}^{*}-\hat{\beta}_{0,s})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{A_{i}(Y_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*})}{\hat{\pi}_{S_{i}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{(1-A_{i})(Y_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*})}{1-\hat{\pi}_{S_{i}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*}-\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*})+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

where the last equality holds because

\displaystyle\max_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}X_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{s}}X_{i}\right\|_{2}=O_{P}((k_{n}/n)^{1/2}),

\displaystyle\max_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}}\right),

by Lemma
G.3
,
and
k_{n}^{2}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})=o(n)
. Let
\delta_{Y,a,s,i}=Y_{i}(a)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)
and
\delta_{X,s,i}=X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{adj}-\tau)\\
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{unadj}-\tau)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}(\delta_{Y,1,s,i}-(1-\hat{\pi}_{s})\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\hat{\pi}_{s}\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*})\\
\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\delta_{Y,1,s,i}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}(\delta_{Y,0,s,i}-\hat{\pi}_{s}\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}-(1-\hat{\pi}_{s})\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*})\\
\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\delta_{Y,0,s,i}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}=s)-\tau)1_{2}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}(\delta_{Y,1,s,i}-(1-\pi_{s})\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\pi_{s}\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*})\\
\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\delta_{Y,1,s,i}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}(\delta_{Y,0,s,i}-\pi_{s}\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}-(1-\pi_{s})\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*})\\
\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\delta_{Y,0,s,i}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i})-\tau)1_{2}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\equiv\tilde{U}_{n,1}-\tilde{U}_{n,0}+W_{n}1_{2}+o_{P}(1),

where we denote

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}(\delta_{Y,1,s,i}-(1-\pi_{s})\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\pi_{s}\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*})\\
\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\delta_{Y,1,s,i}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{n,0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}(\delta_{Y,0,s,i}-\pi_{s}\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}-(1-\pi_{s})\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*})\\
\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\delta_{Y,0,s,i}\end{pmatrix},

W_{n}
is defined in (
B.3
), and the second equality is by the facts that

\displaystyle\hat{\pi}_{s}-\pi_{s}=\frac{\sum_{i\in[n]}(A_{i}-\pi_{s})1\{S_{i}=s\}}{n_{s}}=o_{P}(1),\quad\left|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\delta_{X,s,i}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*})\right|=O_{P}(n^{-1/2}),\quad\text{and thus,}

\displaystyle\max_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}\left|(\hat{\pi}_{s}-\pi_{s})\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\delta_{X,s,i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*})\right)\right|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

Following the same argument by
Bugni
et al. 2018
,
11
11

11

Also see Lemma N.3 in
Jiang
et al. 2023
for a similar argument for the regression adjusted quantile treatment effect estimator.
we can show that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Omega_{U,1}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}_{1}\\
\Omega_{U,0}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}_{0}\\
\sigma_{W}^{-1}W\end{pmatrix}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0_{5},I_{5}),

where
0_{5}
is a
5
-dimensional vector of zero,
I_{5}
is a
5\times 5
identity matrix,
m_{a}(x,s)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i}=x,S_{i}=s)
,
\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*}=(1-\pi_{s})\beta_{1,s}+\pi_{s}\beta_{0,s}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\Omega_{U,1}=\mathbb{E}\frac{Var(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{\pi_{s}}\begin{pmatrix}Var(m_{1}(X_{i},s)-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*}\mid S_{i}=s)&cov(m_{1}(X_{i},s)-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*},m_{1}(X_{i},s)\mid S_{i}=s)\\
cov(m_{1}(X_{i},s)-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*},m_{1}(X_{i},s)\mid S_{i}=s)&Var(m_{1}(X_{i},s)),\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\Omega_{U,0}=\mathbb{E}\frac{Var(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i})}{1-\Pi_{S_{i}}}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{1-\pi_{s}}\begin{pmatrix}Var(m_{0}(X_{i},s)-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*}\mid S_{i}=s)&cov(m_{0}(X_{i},s)-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*},m_{0}(X_{i},s)\mid S_{i}=s)\\
cov(m_{0}(X_{i},s)-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*},m_{0}(X_{i},s)\mid S_{i}=s)&Var(m_{0}(X_{i},s))\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\sigma_{W}^{2}=var(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i})).

We further note that

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle=\Omega_{U,1}+\Omega_{U,0}+Var(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}))1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{Var(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{Var(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i})}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}\right]+\mathbb{E}(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-\tau)^{2}\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{\pi_{s}(1-\pi_{s})}\begin{pmatrix}V_{s}&V_{s}\\
V_{s}&V_{s}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

where

\displaystyle V_{s}=Var((1-\pi_{s})m_{1}(X_{i},s)+\pi_{s}m_{0}(X_{i},s)-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*}\mid S_{i}=s)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle V_{s}^{\prime}=Var((1-\pi_{s})m_{1}(X_{i},s)+\pi_{s}m_{0}(X_{i},s)\mid S_{i}=s).

Then, by the definition of
\beta_{a,s}^{*}
, we see that
V_{s}^{\prime}\geq V_{s}
for
s\in\mathcal{S}
and thus,
\Omega_{1,1}=\Omega_{1,2}\leq\Omega_{2,2}
.

Last, we have

\displaystyle\Omega^{-1/2}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{adj}-\tau)\\
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{unadj}-\tau)\end{pmatrix}=\Omega^{-1/2}\begin{pmatrix}\Omega_{U,1}^{1/2}&\Omega_{U,0}^{1/2}&\sigma_{W}1_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\Omega_{U,1}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}_{1}\\
\Omega_{U,0}^{-1/2}\tilde{U}_{0}\\
\sigma_{W}^{-1}W\end{pmatrix}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0_{2},I_{2})

Step 2: Limit of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{U}}
.

We have

\displaystyle\gamma_{a,s,n}=1-\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}1,

where we use the fact that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)\right]^{-1}\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(k_{n}/n)=o_{P}(1).

In addition, we have
\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,j}=1-R_{a,s,i}
where

R_{a,s,i}=\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right)

so that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}|R_{a,s,i}|\leq\max_{i\in[n]}||\breve{X}_{i}||_{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\right\|_{2}=O_{P}(\sqrt{k_{n}^{2}\xi_{n}/n})=o_{P}(1).

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[\left(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,j}\right)^{2}-1\right]Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}\right|\leq 2\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left(\left|R_{a,s,i}\right|+R_{a,s,i}^{2}\right)\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left|Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}\right|=o_{P}(1),

where we use the facts that

\displaystyle\min_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,i}\geq 1-\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\right)\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}||\breve{X}_{i}||_{2}^{2}=1-o_{P}(1)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left|Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n_{a,s}\min_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,i}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}|Y_{i}||Y_{i}-\hat{\tau}_{a,s}-\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n_{a,s}\min_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,i}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[Y_{i}^{2}+|Y_{i}||\hat{\tau}_{a,s}|+|Y_{i}|||\breve{X}_{i}||_{2}||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}+|Y_{i}||\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|\right]

\displaystyle=O_{P}(1).

To see the last equality of the last display, we note that
\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}^{2}=O_{P}(1)
,
\hat{\tau}_{a,s}=O_{P}(1)
as shown in (
F
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}|Y_{i}|||\breve{X}_{i}||_{2}||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||Y_{i}||_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}||\breve{X}_{i}||_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(1\times\sqrt{k_{n}}\times\sqrt{\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}}\right)=o_{P}(1),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}|Y_{i}||\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|

\displaystyle\leq||Y_{i}||_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}||\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(1\times||\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}\right)=O_{P}(1),

where we use the fact that

\displaystyle||\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(Var(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))||Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)||_{2}\leq C||Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)||_{2}

and by Assumption
5
and
Tripathi 1999

\displaystyle\infty>C

\displaystyle\geq Var(Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)

\displaystyle\geq Cov(X_{i}^{\top},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)(Var(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{-1}Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\max}^{-1}(Var(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))||Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)||_{2}^{2}\geq c||Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)||_{2}^{2},

implying
||Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)||_{2}^{2}
, and thus,
||\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}
are O(1).

Next, recall
\hat{\tau}_{a,s}
and
\hat{\beta}_{a,s}
are the intercept and slope of the OLS regression of
Y_{i}
on
(1,\breve{X}_{i})
using observations
i\in\aleph_{a,s}
. Therefore, by Lemma
G.3
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*})=O_{P}(\sqrt{k_{n}/n})||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}=o_{P}(1),

\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}=o_{P}(1),

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{a,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(Y_{i}-\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}+\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i}=s),

(F.1)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}(\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}-\hat{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i})\right|\leq\left(\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left|Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}\right|\right)=o_{P}(1),

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}\hat{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}(Y_{i}-\hat{\tau}_{a,s}-\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{a,s})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}\right)\hat{\tau}_{a,s}

\displaystyle-\left[\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}X_{i}-\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{s}}X_{i}\right)\right]^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}

\displaystyle-\left[\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}X_{i}-\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{s}}X_{i}\right)\right]^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*})

\displaystyle=Var(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)+o_{P}(1)-cov(Y_{i}(a),X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)-O_{P}(k_{n}^{1/2})||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle=Var(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)-cov(Y_{i}(a),X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=Var(Y_{i}(a)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)+o_{P}(1).

Therefore, we have
\hat{\omega}^{2}_{a,s}=Var(Y_{i}(a)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)+o_{P}(1)
. By a similar argument, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\varpi}_{a,s}=Var(Y_{i}(a)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)+o_{P}(1).

This implies

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{a=0,1}\frac{n_{s}^{2}}{nn_{a,s}}\hat{\omega}_{a,s}^{2}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\left[\frac{Var(Y_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta^{*}_{1,s}|S_{i}=s)}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{Var(Y_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta^{*}_{0,s}|S_{i}=s)}{1-\pi_{s}}\right]+o_{P}(1)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{a=0,1}\frac{n_{s}^{2}}{nn_{a,s}}\hat{\varpi}_{a,s}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\left[\frac{Var(Y_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta^{*}_{1,s}|S_{i}=s)}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{Var(Y_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta^{*}_{0,s}|S_{i}=s)}{1-\pi_{s}}\right]+o_{P}(1).

Step 3: Limit of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{V}}^{adj}
.
We first note that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}\right|\leq\left(\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}|Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}|\right)=o_{P}(1).

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}))^{2}\right)^{1/2}=o_{P}(1),

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\breve{X}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*})^{2}=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\Phi_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{a}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{s}}\Phi_{i})^{2}=Var(\Phi_{i}|S_{i}=s)+o_{P}(1),

where
\Phi_{i}=X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}
.
This implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{a,s}\hat{\beta}_{a,s}=Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{a,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)+o_{P}(1).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{s}}\hat{\beta}_{1,s}^{\top}\Gamma_{s}\hat{\beta}_{0,s}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left[(\hat{\beta}_{1,s}+\hat{\beta}_{0,s})^{\top}\Gamma_{s}(\hat{\beta}_{1,s}+\hat{\beta}_{0,s})-(\hat{\beta}_{1,s}-\hat{\beta}_{0,s})^{\top}\Gamma_{s}(\hat{\beta}_{1,s}-\hat{\beta}_{0,s})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left[Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}+\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)-Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right]+o_{P}(1).

Plug the above results into the expression of
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{V}}^{adj}
and obtain

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{V}}^{adj}=\mathbb{E}Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i})+o_{P}(1).

Step 4:
\hat{\Sigma}^{-1}\Omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}I_{2}.
We have already shown in
Step 1
that
\hat{\tau}_{a,s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)
. This implies
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{W}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}var(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i})).
Therefore, combining results in
Steps 2 and 3
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}=

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\left[\frac{Var(Y_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta^{*}_{1,s}|S_{i}=s)}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{Var(Y_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta^{*}_{0,s}|S_{i}=s)}{1-\pi_{s}}+\right]\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*}-\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*})|S_{i})&\mathbb{E}Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*}-\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*})|S_{i})\\
\mathbb{E}Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*}-\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*})|S_{i})&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,S_{i}}^{*}|S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,S_{i}}^{*}|S_{i})}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}\right]\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+var(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}))1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\left\{\frac{\mathbb{E}[Var(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s]}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}[Var(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s]}{1-\pi_{s}}\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var((1-\pi_{s})(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var(\pi_{s}(m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\left\{Var(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)+Var(m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)-2cov(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*},X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}\mid S_{i}=s)\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}|S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i})}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}-Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}))\right]\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+var(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}))1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\left\{\frac{\mathbb{E}[Var(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s]}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}[Var(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s]}{1-\pi_{s}}\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-\tau)^{2}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var((1-\pi_{s})m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})+\pi_{s}m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right\}1_{2}1_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}|S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i})}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}-Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}))\right]\end{pmatrix}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\Omega+o_{P}(1),

where the first equality holds by the facts that

\displaystyle\frac{Var(Y_{i}(1)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)}{\pi_{s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Var(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s]}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{Var(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)}{\pi_{s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Var(Y_{i}(1)|X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s]}{\pi_{s}}+\frac{Var((1-\pi_{s})(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}

\displaystyle+Var(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)-Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s),

\displaystyle\frac{Var(Y_{i}(0)-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)}{1-\pi_{s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Var(Y_{i}(0)|X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s]}{1-\pi_{s}}+\frac{Var(\pi_{s}(m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}

\displaystyle+Var(m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)-Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s),

and

\displaystyle Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)-Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)-Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)

\displaystyle=-2cov(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*},X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)

\displaystyle=2cov(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*},m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)

\displaystyle-2cov(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i}),m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s),

the second equality holds by the fact that

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var((1-\pi_{s})m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})+\pi_{s}m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var((1-\pi_{s})(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var(\pi_{s}(m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right\}

\displaystyle+2cov(m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*},m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}=s),

and the last equality holds by the fact that

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var((1-\pi_{s})m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})+\pi_{s}m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})|S_{i}=s)\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1,s}^{*}|S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{Var(X_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i})}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}-Var(X_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1,s}^{*}-\beta_{0,s}^{*}|S_{i}))\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{(1-\pi_{s})\pi_{s}}\left\{Var((1-\pi_{s})m_{1}(X_{i},S_{i})+\pi_{s}m_{0}(X_{i},S_{i})-X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*})|S_{i}=s)\right\}.

Given
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\lambda_{\min}(\Omega)>0
, this concludes the proof of
\hat{\Sigma}^{-1}\Omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}I_{2}
.

Step 5: Asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}^{*}
.

We see that
\Omega_{1,1}=\Omega_{1,2}
and

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}(\Omega_{2,2}-\Omega_{1,1})=\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{\pi_{s}(1-\pi_{s})}(V_{s}^{\prime}-V_{s})=\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{p_{s}}{\pi_{s}(1-\pi_{s})}Var(X_{i}^{\top}\overline{\beta}_{s}^{*}|S_{i}=s)>0.

This implies
\hat{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}1
, and thus,

\displaystyle|\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{*}-\hat{\tau}^{adj})|\leq|1-\hat{w}|\left[|\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{adj}-\tau)|+|\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}^{unadj}-\tau)|\right]=o_{P}(1).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left[(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})\hat{\Sigma}(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})^{\top}\right]^{-1/2}(\hat{\tau}^{*}-\tau)\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1)

and
(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})\hat{\Sigma}(\hat{w},1-\hat{w})^{\top}=\Omega_{1,1}+o_{P}(1)<\Omega_{2,2}
.

Appendix G
Technical Lemmas

Lemma G.1
.

Recall the definitions of
\mathcal{U}_{n}
,
\mathcal{V}_{n}
, and
\mathcal{W}_{n}
in (
B.1
), (
B.2
), and (
B.3
), respectively. Suppose Assumptions
1
–
3
holds.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\omega_{1,S_{i},\infty}^{2}}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{\omega_{0,S_{i},\infty}^{2}}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}\right)&\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\varpi_{1,S_{i},\infty}}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{\varpi_{0,S_{i},\infty}}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}\right)\\
\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\varpi_{1,S_{i},\infty}}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{\varpi_{0,S_{i},\infty}}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}\right)&\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(0)|S_{i})}{1-\pi_{S_{i}}}\right)\end{pmatrix}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{n}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}var(\phi_{i}(1)-\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})&\mathbb{E}var(\phi_{i}(1)-\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})\\
\mathbb{E}var(\phi_{i}(1)-\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{var(\phi_{i}(1)|S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{var(\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})}{(1-\pi_{S_{i}})}\right]\end{pmatrix}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{n}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{W}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{N}(0,var(\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(1)|S_{i})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(0)|S_{i}))).

In addition,
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W})
are independent.

Proof.

Following
Bugni
et al. 2018
, we define
\{(X_{i}^{s},\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1),\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(0)):1\leq i\leq n\}
as a sequence of i.i.d. random variables with marginal distributions equal to the distribution of
(X_{i},\varepsilon_{i}(1),\varepsilon_{i}(0))|S_{i}=s
and
N_{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}1\{S_{i}<s\}
. We order units by strata and then by
A_{i}=1
first and
A_{i}=0
second within each stratum. This means for the
s
th stratum, units indexed from
N_{s}+1
to
N_{s}+n_{1,s}
are treated and units indexed from
N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1
to
N_{s}+n_{s}
are untreated. Then, we have
(Y_{i}(a)\mid S_{i}=s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}Y_{i}^{s}(a)
where

\displaystyle Y_{i}^{s}(a)=\mu_{a}(X_{i}^{s},s)+\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(a)\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{a}(x,s)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i}=x,S_{i}=s).

We further define
\tilde{M}_{a,s,i,j}
as the
(i,j)
th entry of the
n_{a,s}\times n_{a,s}
matrix,
