\tilde{M}_{a,s}=I_{n_{a,s}}-\Xi_{a,s}(\Xi_{a,s}^{\top}\Xi_{a,s})^{-1}\Xi_{a,s}^{\top}
,
\Xi_{1,s}=((\breve{X}_{N_{s}+1}^{s})^{\top},\cdots,(\breve{X}_{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}^{s})^{\top})^{\top}
is an
n_{1,s}\times k_{n}
matrix,
\Xi_{0,s}=((\breve{X}_{N_{s}+1+n_{1,s}}^{s})^{\top},\cdots,(\breve{X}_{N_{s}+n_{s}}^{s})^{\top})^{\top}
is an
n_{0,s}\times k_{n}
matrix,

\displaystyle\breve{X}_{i}^{s}=X_{i}^{s}-\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}X_{j}^{s},\quad\text{if}\quad N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{s},

\tilde{\gamma}_{a,s,n}=1_{n_{a,s}}^{\top}\tilde{M}_{a,s}1_{n_{a,s}}
, and
\phi_{i}^{s}(a)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}^{s}(a)|X_{i}^{s})-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}^{s}(a))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|S_{i}=s)
.

Conditional on
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)})
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{U}_{n},\mathcal{V}_{n})|(A^{(n)},S^{(n)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n})|(A^{(n)},S^{(n)}),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}n_{1,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left(\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,j}\right)\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1)\right]\\
-\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-1}n_{0,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\left(\sum_{j=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\tilde{M}_{0,s,i,j}\right)\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(0)\right]\\
n_{1,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1)\right]-n_{0,s}^{-1}\left[\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(0)\right]\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\hat{p}_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\left[\phi_{i}^{s}(1)-\phi_{i}^{s}(0)\right]\\
\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\phi_{i}^{s}(1)-\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\phi_{i}^{s}(0)\end{pmatrix}.

In addition, because
\mathcal{W}_{n}
only depends on
S^{(n)}
, it implies the joint distribution of
(\mathcal{U}_{n},\mathcal{V}_{n},\mathcal{W}_{n})
and
(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n},\mathcal{W}_{n})
are the same, i.e.,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{U}_{n},\mathcal{V}_{n},\mathcal{W}_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n},\mathcal{W}_{n}).

(G.1)

We aim to show that, for any
t_{1}\in\Re^{2}
,
t_{2}\in\Re^{2}
, and
t_{3}\in\Re
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}_{n}\leq t_{1},\mathcal{V}_{n}\leq t_{2},\mathcal{W}_{n}\leq t_{3}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{W}\leq t_{3}\right)\right|=o(1).

By the above definition, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}_{n}\leq t_{1},\mathcal{V}_{n}\leq t_{2},\mathcal{W}_{n}\leq t_{3}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{W}\leq t_{3}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2},\mathcal{W}_{n}\leq t_{3}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{W}\leq t_{3}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)1\{\mathcal{W}_{n}\leq t_{3}\}-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{W}\leq t_{3}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\right|+|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{n}\leq t_{3})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}\leq t_{3})|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\right|+o(1),

where first equality is by (
G.1
) and the second inequality is because by the Lindeberg central limit theorem,
\mathcal{W}_{n}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{W}
. Therefore, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

Let
\Xi^{(n)}=\{X_{i}^{s}\}_{i\in[n],s\in\mathcal{S}}
. Then,
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
belongs to the sigma field generated by
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)})
. We have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\right|\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right]+\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\right|.

(G.2)

We show the two terms on the RHS of (
G.2
) vanish in probability in the following two steps.

Step 1: The first term on the RHS of (
G.2
)
. It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)=o_{P}(1).

Let

\displaystyle Z_{i}=\begin{cases}\left((n_{s}/n_{1,s})\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1),(n_{s}/n_{1,s})\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1)\right)^{\top}&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}\\
\left((n_{s}/n_{0,s})\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(0),(n_{s}/n_{0,s})\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(0)\right)^{\top}&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{s}\end{cases},

where

\displaystyle\theta_{i}=\begin{cases}\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,j}&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}\\
\sum_{j=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\tilde{M}_{0,s,i,j}&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{s}\end{cases}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in[n]}Z_{i}.

By construction,

\displaystyle\max_{N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\left|\sum_{j=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{M}_{1,s,i,j}\right|\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\left|\sum_{j\in\aleph_{1,s}}M_{1,s,i,j}\right|

share the same distribution conditional on
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)})
, and thus, unconditionally. Same for

\displaystyle\max_{N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{s}}\left|\sum_{j=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\tilde{M}_{0,s,i,j}\right|\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\left|\sum_{j\in\aleph_{0,s}}M_{0,s,i,j}\right|.

Therefore, Assumption
2
(v) implies

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}|\theta_{i}|=o_{P}(n^{1/2})

(G.3)

In addition, we note that
\left(\hat{p}_{s},n_{1,s},n_{0,s},n_{s},\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n},\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n},\theta_{i}\right)
belong to the sigma field generated by
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)})
and
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
are independent and mean-zero conditional on
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)})
. Define

\displaystyle H_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\left(Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\top}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\biggl[\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\begin{pmatrix}(n_{s}/n_{1,s})^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}\theta_{i}^{2}\eta_{i}^{2}&(n_{s}/n_{1,s})^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}\\
(n_{s}/n_{1,s})^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}&(n_{s}/n_{1,s})^{2}\eta_{i}^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\begin{pmatrix}(n_{s}/n_{0,s})^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-2}\theta_{i}^{2}\eta_{i}^{2}&(n_{s}/n_{0,s})^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}\\
(n_{s}/n_{0,s})^{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}&(n_{s}/n_{0,s})^{2}\eta_{i}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\biggr],

where

\displaystyle\eta_{i}^{2}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}\left[(\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(1))^{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right]&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[(\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(0))^{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right]&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{s}\end{cases}.

By construction, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{i}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{1,s}\\
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(0)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)&\text{if}\quad N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1\leq i\leq N_{s}+n_{s}\end{cases}.

Further define
Z_{i}=(Z_{i,1},Z_{i,2})^{\top}
and

\displaystyle L_{n}=\max_{\ell=1,2}\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\left(|Z_{i,\ell}^{3}|\mid A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right).

Then, we have

\displaystyle L_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{n}^{3}}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\biggl[(n/n_{1,s})^{3}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-3}|\theta_{i}|^{3}+(n/n_{0,s})^{3}\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\gamma_{0,s,n}^{-3}|\theta_{i}|^{3}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\overline{\sigma}^{3}_{n}n^{3}}{\min_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}n_{a,s}^{3}\min_{a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}\tilde{\gamma}_{a,s,n}^{2}}\frac{\max_{i\in[n]}|\theta_{i}|}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{a,s,n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\gamma_{a,s,n}
,

\overline{\sigma}_{n}^{3}=\max_{i\in[n],a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\varepsilon_{i}^{s}(a)|^{3}\mid A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\max_{i\in[n],a=0,1,s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{i}^{3}(a)|\mid X_{i},S_{i}=s),

the second inequality holds by the facts that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}|\theta_{i}|^{3}\leq\max_{i\in[n]}|\theta_{i}|\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}|\theta_{i}|^{2}=\max_{i\in[n]}|\theta_{i}|\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}|\theta_{i}|^{3}\leq\max_{i\in[n]}|\theta_{i}|\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}|\theta_{i}|^{2}=\max_{i\in[n]}|\theta_{i}|\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n},

and the last convergence is by (
G.3
).

By the Yurinskii’s coupling (
Pollard 2002
), there exists a version of
\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
and a universal constant
C_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\mid A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{N}(0,H_{n})

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}-\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\right\|_{2}\geq 3\delta_{n}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)\leq C_{0}2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}\left(1+\frac{|\log(1/(2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}))|}{2}\right).

(G.4)

Because
L_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
, we can choose
\delta_{n}=o(1)
such that
L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}=o(1)
, which implies

C_{0}2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}\left(1+\frac{|\log(1/(2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}))|}{2}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

and thus,

\displaystyle\left\|\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}-\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\right\|_{2}=o_{P}(1).

Furthermore, note that

\displaystyle H_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n_{s}^{2}}{nn_{1,s}}\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{1,s}}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-2}\theta_{i}^{2}\eta_{i}^{2}&\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}\\
\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}&\eta_{i}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n_{s}^{2}}{nn_{0,s}}\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i=N_{s}+n_{1,s}+1}^{N_{s}+n_{s}}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-2}\theta_{i}^{2}\eta_{i}^{2}&\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}\\
\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-1}\theta_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}&\eta_{i}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n_{s}^{2}}{nn_{1,s}}\biggl[\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-2}\sigma_{1,s,n}^{2}&\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}\rho_{1,s,n}\\
\gamma_{1,s,n}^{-1}\rho_{1,s,n}&\tilde{\gamma}_{1,s,n}^{-1}\frac{1}{n_{1,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{1,s}}\left[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)\right]\end{pmatrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{n_{s}^{2}}{nn_{0,s}}\biggl[\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{0,s,n}^{-2}\sigma_{0,s,n}^{2}&\tilde{\gamma}_{0,s,n}^{-1}\rho_{0,s,n}\\
\gamma_{0,s,n}^{-1}\rho_{0,s,n}&\frac{1}{n_{0,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{0,s}}\left[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(0)|X_{i},S_{i}=s)\right]\end{pmatrix}\biggr]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\left[\frac{p_{s}}{\pi_{s}}\begin{pmatrix}\omega_{1,s,n}^{2}&\varpi_{1,s,n}\\
\varpi_{1,s,n}&\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(1)|S_{i}=s)\end{pmatrix}+\frac{p_{s}}{1-\pi_{s}}\begin{pmatrix}\omega_{0,s,n}^{2}&\varpi_{0,s,n}\\
\varpi_{0,s,n}&\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(0)|S_{i}=s)\end{pmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=H,

where the second last line holds by Assumption
3
and
H
is nonrandom.

We can write
\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}=H^{1/2}_{n}\mathcal{G}_{2}
, where
\mathcal{G}_{2}
is a two-dimensional standard Gaussian vector such that
\mathcal{G}_{2}\perp\!\!\!\perp A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}
. Further denote
\mathcal{U}=H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}
and note that

\displaystyle||H^{1/2}_{n}-H^{1/2}||_{op}\leq||H_{n}-H||_{op}^{1/2}=o_{P}(1),

where the inequality is by
Bhatia 2013
with
f(u)=u^{1/2}
. Let
F_{n}(\delta^{\prime})=\{||H_{n}-H||_{op}^{1/2}\leq(\delta^{\prime})^{2}\}
for any
(\delta^{\prime})^{2}>0
. Then,
\mathbb{P}(F_{n}(\delta^{\prime}))\rightarrow 1
and
F_{n}(\delta^{\prime})
belongs to the sigma field generated by
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)})
. On
F_{n}(\delta^{\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(U\leq t_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(H_{n}^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1}+1_{2}||H^{1/2}_{n}-H^{1/2}||_{op}||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1}+1_{2}\delta^{\prime}\right)-\mathbb{P}(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1})+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime}).

By Assumption
2
(ii), we see that
H_{1,1}
and
H_{2,2}
are bounded above from zero. Therefore, by
Chernozhukov et al. 2014
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1}+1_{2}\delta^{\prime}\right)-\mathbb{P}(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}\leq t_{1})\leq C\delta^{\prime},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)\leq C\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime}).

By a similar argument, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}>t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}>t_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(H_{n}^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}>t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}>t_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}>t_{1}-1_{2}||H^{1/2}_{n}-H^{1/2}||_{op}||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}>t_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}>t_{1}-1_{2}\delta^{\prime}\right)-\mathbb{P}(H^{1/2}\mathcal{G}_{2}>t_{1})+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime}).

Combining these two bounds, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)\right|\leq C\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime})+1\{F_{n}^{c}(\delta^{\prime})\}.

By letting
n\rightarrow\infty
( so that
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
) followed by
\delta^{\prime}\downarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime})+1\{F_{n}^{c}(\delta^{\prime})\}+\mathbb{P}\left(t_{1}-3\delta_{n}1_{2}\leq\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}+3\delta_{n}1_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}-\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\right\|_{2}\geq 3\delta_{n}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime})+\mathbb{P}\left(t_{1}-3\delta_{n}1_{2}\leq\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}+3\delta_{n}1_{2}\right)+1\{F_{n}^{c}(\delta^{\prime})\}\right]

\displaystyle+C_{0}2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}\left(1+\frac{|\log(1/(2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}))|}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime})+\delta_{n}+1\{F_{n}^{c}(\delta^{\prime})\}\right]+C_{0}2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}\left(1+\frac{|\log(1/(2L_{n}\delta_{n}^{-3}))|}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

where the third inequality is by (
G.4
) and fact that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(t_{1}-3\delta_{n}1_{2}\leq\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}+3\delta_{n}1_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(t_{1}-3\delta_{n}1_{2}\leq\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}+3\delta_{n}1_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}+3\delta_{n}1_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}+3\delta_{n}1_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\breve{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\leq t_{1}-3\delta_{n}1_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},\Xi^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{U}\leq t_{1}-3\delta_{n}1_{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(||\mathcal{G}_{2}||_{2}\geq 1/\delta^{\prime})+2\cdot 1\{F_{n}^{c}(\delta^{\prime})\},

and the fourth inequality is by
Chernozhukov et al. 2014
.

Step 2: The second term on the RHS of (
G.2
)
. We first define

\displaystyle\tilde{V}^{*}_{n}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p_{s}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{np_{s}}\sum_{i=\lfloor n\mathbb{P}(S<s)\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n\mathbb{P}(S\leq s)\rfloor}\left[\phi_{i}^{s}(1)-\mathbb{E}(\phi_{i}^{s}(1))-\left(\phi_{i}^{s}(0)-\mathbb{E}(\phi_{i}^{s}(0))\right)\right]\\
\frac{1}{n\pi_{s}p_{s}}\sum_{i=\lfloor n\mathbb{P}(S<s)\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(\mathbb{P}(S<s)+\pi_{s}p_{s})\rfloor}(\phi_{i}^{s}(1)-\mathbb{E}(\phi_{i}^{s}(1)))\\
-\frac{1}{n(1-\pi_{s})p_{s}}\sum_{i=\lfloor n(\mathbb{P}(S<s)+\pi_{s}p_{s})\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(\mathbb{P}(S\leq s))\rfloor}(\phi_{i}^{s}(0)-\mathbb{E}(\phi_{i}^{s}(0)))\end{pmatrix},

we note that

\displaystyle N_{s}/n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}(S<s),\quad n_{1,s}/n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi_{s}p_{s},\quad\text{and}\quad n_{0,s}/n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}(1-\pi_{s})p_{s}.

Because the partial sum process is stochastically equicontinuous, we have

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}+o_{P}(1)
. By construction, we have
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}\perp\!\!\!\perp(A^{(n)},X^{(n)})
, and by the Lindeberg CLT,
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}\rightsquigarrow V
. In particular, we see that the limit distribution of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}p^{2}(s)\begin{pmatrix}\frac{var(\phi_{i}^{s}(1)-\phi_{i}^{s}(0))}{p_{s}}&\frac{var(\phi_{i}^{s}(1)-\phi_{i}^{s}(0))}{p_{s}}\\
\frac{var(\phi_{i}^{s}(1)-\phi_{i}^{s}(0))}{p_{s}}&\frac{var(\phi_{i}^{s}(1))}{\pi_{s}p_{s}}+\frac{var(\phi_{i}^{s}(0))}{(1-\pi_{s})p_{s}}\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}var(\phi_{i}(1)-\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})&\mathbb{E}var(\phi_{i}(1)-\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})\\
\mathbb{E}var(\phi_{i}(1)-\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{var(\phi_{i}(1)|S_{i})}{\pi_{S_{i}}}+\frac{var(\phi_{i}(0)|S_{i})}{(1-\pi_{S_{i}})}\right]\end{pmatrix}\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}\leq t_{2}+\delta\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(|\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}|\geq\delta\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}\leq t_{2}+\delta\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(|\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}|\geq\delta\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}\leq t_{2}+\delta\right)+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}+\delta\right)+o_{P}(1),

where the first equality is by the fact that
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}
is independent of
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)})
and the second equality holds because by Markov’s inequality, for any
\delta^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(|\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}|\geq\delta\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)\geq\delta^{\prime}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{*}|\geq\delta\right)}{\delta^{\prime}}\rightarrow 0.

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}>t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}>t_{2}-\delta\right)+o_{P}(1),

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}-\delta\right)+o_{P}(1).

By letting
n\rightarrow\infty
followed by
\delta\downarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\leq t_{2}\bigg|A^{(n)},S^{(n)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{V}\leq t_{2}\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

This concludes the proof.

∎

Lemma G.2
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
–
4
hold. Then, we have

\varepsilon_{\aleph_{a,s}}^{\top}(a)P_{a,s}\varepsilon_{\aleph_{a,s}}(a)-\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}=o_{P}(n).

Proof.

Let
H_{a,s,i,j}=M_{a,s,i,j}-(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,j})\gamma_{a,s,\infty}^{-1}(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,j})
and
H_{a,s}
be the
n_{a,s}\times n_{a,s}
matrix with its
(i,j)
th entry being
H_{a,s,i,j}
. We can see that
\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}H_{a,s,i,j}H_{a,s,j,k}=H_{a,s,i,k}
, which means
H_{a,s}
is idempotent. In addition, we have
\hat{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}=\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}H_{a,s,i,j}(e_{j,s}(a)+\varepsilon_{j}(a))
and
\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i}/M_{a,s,i,i}
. Further denote
\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,j}=H_{a,s,i,j}/M_{a,s,i,i}
and
\mu_{i}(a)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i})
. This implies

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s},j\neq i}\varepsilon_{i}(a)P_{a,s,i,j}\varepsilon_{j}(a)+\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(a)-Y_{i}\acute{\varepsilon}_{a,s,i})

\displaystyle=\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(a)(1-\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,i})\right]+\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s},j\neq i}\varepsilon_{i}(a)P_{a,s,i,j}\varepsilon_{i}(a)\right]

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s},j\neq i}P_{a,s,i,i}\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,j}\varepsilon_{i}(a)\varepsilon_{j}(a)\right]-\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,j}\mu_{i}(a)e_{j,s}(a)\right]

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,j}\mu_{i}(a)\varepsilon_{j}(a)\right]-\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,j}\varepsilon_{i}(a)e_{j,s}(a)\right]

\displaystyle\equiv I_{1,1}+I_{1,2}-I_{1,3}-I_{1,4}-I_{1,5}-I_{1,6}.

Conditional on
(A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})
,
\aleph_{a,s}
,
P_{a,s}
,
\tilde{H}_{a,s}
,
\phi_{i}(a)
, and
e_{i,s}(a)
are all deterministic and
\{\varepsilon_{i}(a)\}_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}
are independent across
i
, conditionally mean zero, and
\varepsilon_{i}(a)|(A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\varepsilon_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i}=s
. For
I_{1,1}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{1,1}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(a)\frac{(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,j})^{2}}{1_{\aleph_{a,s}}^{\top}M_{a,s}1_{\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,i}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(a)\right)\left(\frac{\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,j})^{2}}{n}\right)\left(\frac{n}{n_{a,s}\gamma_{a,s,n}\min_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,i}}\right)=o_{P}(n)

where we use Assumptions
2
,
3
,
4
, and the facts that
\min_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,i}\geq\delta>0
and

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\varepsilon_{i}^{2}(a)\right)=O_{P}(n).

For
I_{1,2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(I_{1,2}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=0

and

\displaystyle var(I_{1,2}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s},j\neq i}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{i}(a)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})P_{a,s,i,j}^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{j}(a)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,j}^{2}\max_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i})=O_{P}(n),

which implies

\displaystyle I_{1,2}=O_{P}(n^{1/2})=o_{P}(n).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(I_{1,3}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=0

and

\displaystyle var(I_{1,3}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,j}^{2}P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}\max_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}H_{a,s,i,j}^{2}(\min_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}M_{a,s,i,i})^{-2}\max_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{i}(a)|X_{i},S_{i})

\displaystyle=O_{P}(n),

where the second inequality holds by the fact that
P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}\leq 1
and the last equality holds because
H_{a,s}
is idempotent. This implies
I_{1,3}=o_{P}(n)
.

For
I_{1,4}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1,4}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}\mu_{i}^{2}(a)M_{a,s,i,i}^{-2}\right)\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}H_{a,s,i,j}e_{j,s}(a)\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}M_{a,s,i,i}^{-2}\mu_{i}^{2}(a)\right)\left(\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}H_{a,s,j^{\prime},j}e_{j^{\prime},s}(a)e_{j,s}(a)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}M_{a,s,i,i}^{-2}\right)\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mu_{i}^{2}(a)\right)\left(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}e_{j,s}^{2}(a)\right)=o_{P}(n),

where the second inequality is because
H_{a,s}
is idempotent , the last inequality is by
||H_{a,s}||_{op}\leq 1
, and the last equality is by the facts that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mu_{i}^{2}(a)\right)=O_{P}(n),\quad\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}M_{a,s,i,i}^{-2}=O_{P}(1),\quad\left(\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}e_{j,s}^{2}(a)\right)=o_{P}(1).

This implies
I_{1,4}=o_{P}(n^{1/2})=o_{P}(n)
.

For
I_{1,5}
, we have
\mathbb{E}(I_{1,5}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})=0
and

\displaystyle var(I_{1,5}|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}P_{a,s,i,i}\tilde{H}_{a,s,i,j}\mu_{i}(a)\right]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{j}(a)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\frac{P_{a,s,i,i}\mu_{i}(a)}{M_{a,s,i,i}}H_{a,s,i,j}\frac{P_{a,s,j,j}\mu_{j}(a)}{M_{a,s,j,j}}\left[\max_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{j}(a)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\frac{P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}\mu_{i}^{2}(a)}{M_{a,s,i,i}^{2}}\left[\max_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{j}(a)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mu^{2}_{i}(a)\right)\left(\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\frac{P_{a,s,i,i}^{2}}{M_{a,s,i,i}^{2}}\right)\left(\max_{j\in\aleph_{a,s}}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}_{j}(a)|A^{(n)},S^{(n)},X^{(n)})\right)

\displaystyle=O_{P}(n),

where the second inequality is by
||H_{a,s}||_{op}\leq 1
. This implies
I_{1,5}=O_{P}(n^{1/2})=o_{P}(n)
. In the same manner, we can show that
I_{1,6}=o_{P}(n)
, which concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma G.3
.

Suppose Assumptions in Theorem
5.1
hold. Then, we have

\displaystyle||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}}\right).

Proof.

Denote
\overline{X}_{a,s}=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}X_{i}
and
\overline{Y}_{a,s}=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}Y_{i}

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{a,s}=\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{a,s})\right),

\displaystyle\beta_{a,s}^{*}=Var(X_{i}|S_{i}=s)^{-1}Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s).

We first define

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{a,s}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}|S_{i}=s))\right).

We first bound
||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\tilde{\beta}_{a,s}||_{2}
.
Let
S^{k_{n}}
be the unit sphere in
\Re^{k_{n}}
,
\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s}f_{i}=\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}f_{i}
for any function of observations
f_{i}
,
\mathbb{P}_{a,s}f_{i}=\mathbb{E}(f_{i}|A_{i}=a,S_{i}=s)=\mathbb{E}(f_{i}|S_{i}=s)
,
||\cdot||_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}
be the
L_{2}
norm w.r.t. the probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s}
. Note that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}-(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\right]\right\|_{op}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\in S^{k_{n}}}\left|\left\|(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}^{2}-\left\|(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\in S^{k_{n}}}\left|\left\|(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}-\left\|(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}\right|

\displaystyle\times\sup_{\lambda\in S^{k_{n}}}\left|\left\|(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}+\left\|(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\overline{X}_{a,s}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))\right\|_{2}\sup_{\lambda\in S^{k_{n}}}\left|\left\|(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}+\left\|(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(\sqrt{k_{n}/n})\left(1+O_{P}(\sqrt{k_{n}/n})\right)=O_{P}(\sqrt{k_{n}/n}),

(G.5)

where we use the fact that

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in S^{k_{n}}}\left\|(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\lambda\right\|_{\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s},2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\right]\right\|_{op}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(1).

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}|S_{i}=s))-(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{a,s})\right]\right\|_{2}=O_{P}(\sqrt{k_{n}/n}),

(G.6)

implying that

\displaystyle||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\tilde{\beta}_{a,s}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}-(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\right]\right\|_{op}

\displaystyle\times\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}|S_{i}=s))\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})^{\top}\right)\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\left[(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}|S_{i}=s))-(X_{i}-\overline{X}_{a,s})(Y_{i}-\overline{Y}_{a,s})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\sqrt{k_{n}/n}),

where the last equality holds by (
G
), (
G.6
), (
G
) below and the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}|S_{i}=s))\right\|_{2}=\left\|Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)\right\|_{2}+o_{P}(1)=O_{P}(1),

Next, we bound
||\tilde{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}
.
We note that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}-Var(X_{i}|S_{i}=s)\right\|_{op}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\in S^{k_{n}}}\left|(\mathbb{P}_{n,a,s}-\mathbb{P}_{a,s})((X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\lambda)^{2}\right|.

Further denote
M=\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}\sup_{\lambda\in S^{k_{n}}}|(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}\lambda|=\max_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}||X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s)||_{2}
.

Conditioning on
(S_{i},A_{i})_{i\in[n]}
, we can treat
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}
as i.i.d. with distribution
\mathbb{P}_{a,s}
which is just the conditional distribution of
X_{i}
given
S_{i}=s
.

In addition, by
Belloni et al. 2015
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))^{\top}-Var(X_{i}|S_{i}=s)\right\|_{op}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}}+\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}\right)

(G.7)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a,s}}\sum_{i\in\aleph_{a,s}}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|S_{i}=s))(Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}|S_{i}=s))-Cov(X_{i},Y_{i}|S_{i}=s)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}}+\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}\right),

(G.8)

which implies

\displaystyle||\tilde{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}}\right),

and thus,

\displaystyle||\hat{\beta}_{a,s}-\beta_{a,s}^{*}||_{2}=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{k_{n}\xi_{n}^{2}\log(k_{n})}{n}}\right).

∎

H.2
Additional Results for Bias

Table
4
presents the empirical bias of ATE estimators across all cases. The number of regressors is set to
0.4\frac{n}{2|\mathcal{S}|}
, corresponding to
\kappa_{a,s}=0.4
. Figure
10
illustrates how the empirical bias of
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
, YYS, and LTM changes with the number of regressors under both the null and alternative hypotheses and the SBR randomization scheme.

We find that the empirical bias terms are asymptotically negligible in all cases compared to the standard deviations. This finding supports our theoretical results that all treatment effect estimators are asymptotically unbiased. Similar results were observed for other randomization schemes, which are omitted here.

Note: All simulations were conducted with 10,000 replications. The ratio of sample standard

deviations to the average of estimated standard errors is given in parenthesis.

In most cases,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
shows the smallest standard deviations, confirming our theoretical result that
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
is the most efficient combination of
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and the unadjusted estimator. In Model 4, however, when
\kappa_{\alpha,s}
is above
0.3
, LTM achieves a slightly smaller standard deviation. This outcome is consistent with our theory, as the Lasso estimator does not fall within the class of estimators based on
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and
\hat{\tau}^{unadj}
. The main challenge with using Lasso for adjustment is its inability to control size when the sparsity condition is not met, as shown in Table 1 for Model 4 in the main text. Results under other randomization schemes are similar and are therefore omitted.

H.4
Simulation analysis of the sensitivity to violation of Assumption 2(iv)

In this section, we consider two DGPs that violate Assumption 2(iv). Similar to models 1-4, our data generating processes are as follows. For
a\in\{0,1\}
, we generate potential outcomes according to the equation

Y_{i}(a)=\mu_{a}+m_{a}(Z_{i})+\sigma_{a}(Z_{i})\varepsilon_{i}(a),

where
\mu_{a}
, and
m_{a}\left(Z_{i}\right)
and
\sigma_{a}(Z_{i})
are specified as follows. In all specifications,
\{Z_{i},\varepsilon_{i}(1),\varepsilon_{i}(0)\}
are i.i.d. The conditional mean functions
m_{a}(\cdot)
are different for each model.

Model 5: Model with incorrect regressors

The dimension of
Z_{i}
is set to
d_{n}=0.2n/|\mathcal{S}|
. The first entry of
Z_{i}
is uniformly distributed in
[-1,1]
, i.e.,
Z_{1i}\sim U[-1,1]
.
The other entries of
Z_{i}
are
[Z_{2i},\cdots,Z_{d_{n},i}]^{\top}=\Sigma_{\rho}^{1/2}\mathbf{v}_{i}
, where
\mathbf{v}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{n}-1}
containing i.i.d.
U[-1,1]
entries and
\Sigma_{\rho}
is a Toeplitz matrix with

[\Sigma_{\rho}]_{i,j}=\rho^{|i-j|}
and
\rho=0.6
.
The conditional variances of idiosyncratic terms are given by

\sigma_{0}(Z_{i})=\sigma_{1}(Z_{i})=c_{\varepsilon}\left[1+\left(Z_{1i}+\sum_{j=2}^{d_{n}}Z_{ji}/\sqrt{d_{n}-1}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

We set
c_{\varepsilon}
as the normalizing constant such that
\mathbb{E}\sigma_{a}^{2}(Z_{i})=1
. Set
(\varepsilon_{i}(1),\varepsilon_{i}(0))\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{2})
to be independent of
Z_{i}
.

Model 5 is the same as Model 3, but with the conditional means defined as:

m_{1}(Z_{i})=2\sum_{j=2}^{d_{n}}Z_{ji}/\sqrt{d_{n}-1}
, and

m_{0}(Z_{i})=2\sum_{j=2}^{d_{n}}Z_{ji}^{3}/\sqrt{d_{n}-1}.

We directly use
Z_{ji}
’s as regressors so that the regression adjustment for
m_{0}(\cdot)
is not correctly specified.

Model 6:

The setting is the same as Model 5, but with the conditional means defined as:

m_{1}(Z_{i})=2\sum_{j=2}^{d_{n}}Z_{ji}/\sqrt{d_{n}-1}
, and

m_{0}(Z_{i})=2\sum_{j=2}^{d_{n}}\left(\frac{Z_{ji}^{2}-1/3}{3}+\frac{Z_{ji}}{10}\right)/\sqrt{d_{n}-1}
.

We directly use
Z_{ji}
’s as regressors, so that the regression adjustment for
m_{0}(\cdot)
is not correctly specified.

Table
6
presents the test sizes for the estimators, while Table
7
shows their power under
H_{1}:\mu_{1}-\mu_{0}=0.2
. The number of regressors is set to
0.4\frac{n}{2|\mathcal{S}|}
, corresponding to
\kappa_{a,s}=0.4
. The results are similar to those of models 1-4. Specifically, under
H_{0}
,
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
maintain good size control across both models, with accuracy improving as
n
increases. In contrast, YYS and LTM significantly over-reject the null hypothesis. BCS, which does not use any regressors, is unaffected by the size distortion that arises with many regressors. Under
H_{1}
,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
consistently shows higher power than
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and BCS, as predicted by our theory. YYS and LTM exhibit higher rejection rates due to their use of an incorrect variance estimator.

Figures
12
-
13
show the rejection probabilities, bias, and standard deviations of the methods considered, with varying numbers of regressors under the SBR randomization scheme. We examine Models 5 and 6 for
n=800
and
n=400
, setting the number of regressors to
k_{n}=0,2,\ldots,80
for
n=800
and
k_{n}=0,1,\ldots,40
for
n=400
, corresponding to
\kappa_{a,s}=0,0.02,\ldots,0.4
. The
x
-axis represents
\kappa_{a,s}
.

Despite Assumption 2(iv) not holding,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
and
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
perform well. First, panels (a) of the figures indicate that
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
and
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
maintain uniform size control across different values of
\kappa_{a,s}
, with close-to-nominal rejection rates under
H_{0}
, even as the number of regressors ranges from 0 to 80 (40) for
n=800
(
n=400
). In contrast, LTM and YYS lack uniform size control, as their rejection probabilities increase with the number of regressors. Second,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
consistently achieves higher power than both BCS and
\hat{\tau}^{adj}
. Third, we observe that the bias is nearly zero in Model 5 and remains small in Model 6, with a slight increase as
\kappa_{a,s}
grows. However, this bias is asymptotically negligible relative to the standard deviations. Fourth,
\hat{\tau}^{\ast}
has the smallest standard deviation among all methods. Similar results were obtained under other randomization schemes and are omitted here for brevity.
